#5396 1 2005-11-26T10:34:35Z krusch
An idea I had which I think could make a handy thread.
I always find that there are maps which I see on NUMA and tell myself to play, but I forget about them, then I look days later and they're gone.

If you are looking for a map which you've seen or heard about but you can't find in on NUMA or the forums, post you're request here.

In your request you should try to be as descriptive and detailed as possibe.
Try to name the Author, concept, genre etc.

And remember that you can post anyone's map which you have in your computer as long as you don't try and pass it off as your own, or we hurt you.


For starters; I'm looking for a wellsj map which is the same concept as acrophilia by stepself. It has lots of frozen zap-drones in the air. I would really love to get it if anyone has it backed up.
Cheers

#129003 5 2006-01-01T10:55:27Z krusch
Ok, you can play them in the level editor or put them in your userlevels text.

To play them in the editor simply press the "`" key which is found in the top left corner of the keyboard, that will take you into debug mode.
Now paste the data into the level data box, click the cursor outside the box, press "L" to load the level and "P" to play it.
Make sure you have capslock switched on.

To play them in userlevels mode you have to find the text file named "userlevels" (It will be in the same folder as N).
Then you need to scroll to the bottom of the file, paste in the data, and save the changes.
Be careful if you mess around with the syntax or the text up the top because you could mess it all up.

Hope that helped.

#136566 3 2006-01-28T05:29:38Z krusch
Gosh, I don't think you guys are taking to heart what it means to make episodes.
I've been working on making episodes for weeks and I haven't finished 1 yet.

You need to think about every piece of gold.
You need to structure them well and make them atmospheric.
You need to think about how you feel going from map to map and what influences that. Eg. the kinds of enemies or the how the tiles are shaped.

It is alot of of work if you are going to make real episodes.
They are faaaar more complicated than just making maps.
Far more complicated.
I just want to make sure you guys understand that.

- - - - - - - - - -

Parameters and such:
Read them, please.

I was thinking about tktktk's Minimalist Series and an astheoceansblue map by the name of daNse macabre and I felt inspired to start this contest. The theme is gold, mines, tiles. No other enemies or objects (bar the ninja and the exit/exits, of course) are permitted.

Judges are: pariah, Barabajagal and KinGAleX.
Backup judge is: Me, krusch!

Rules are:
- Maps may not contain any objects other than the ninja, the exit/exits, gold, and mines.

- A maximum of two entries per author.

- Maps must be submitted to NUMA and linked in this thread by March 15th.

- Any type of map can be submitted except for N-Art.

- Maps must have been created on, or after, February 12th 2006.

- Keep it friendly; don't be spiteful about anything or anyone.

Judging is based on 2 categories:
- Originality: I don't want to see copies of Bonzai; or any other map, for that matter. (mark out of 30)

- Overall Quality: This includes efficiency of item usage, playability/fun factor, atmosphere and so on. (mark out of 70)


I strongly encourage you to be creative; don't just make generic mine jumpers or races.
Think up some new concepts.
I also encourage you to be careful with your submissions; you only have 2 so use them wisely.

I'll try to keep this post updated and hopefully we'll have a productive and enjoyable contest.
Get crackin'.

Judging parameters changed with help from Sweep, thanks mate.

End!

#155570 2 2006-03-18T11:01:37Z krusch
You'll need to upload it somewhere, I suggest you get an account on FileLodge, which is pretty reliable.

Then you have to upload the image and get the direct link to it, which will look something like: http://www.filelodge.com/files/room15/386751/image.png

Then go to to where you edit your signature and copy in that direct link between two 'tags'. You can type the tags manually or use the button near where you select font/colour etc.

It'll look something like:

[IMG.]http://www.filelodge.com/files/room15/386751/image.png[/IMG.]

Note: I used the full stops so it wouldn't read that as an actual tag. Don't put the full stops in when you want to display an image.


I hope that all works for you.

#9939 1 2006-07-08T04:56:42Z krusch
Welcome.

It seems the day has finally come, and the Project 42 subforum has been unmasked: a new attraction for our precious Metanet Forums' front page.

In March this year, soon after the release of the astounding Legacy Team's first project, a few humble IRC-goers were chatting about making levelpacks. The topic mainly focused on hype, and the amount of hype those authors would generate in comparison to Legacy, if they were to work on a levelpack together.

Soon after, Aju began asking around on IRC, looking for keen mapmakers to work together and form a little mapmaking alliance, with the end product being an episodic levelpack, much like the Legacy.

And so, the team was formed.

9400
Aju
Bigblargh
Evil_bob
KinGAleX
krusch
sept
and spect

Working together, the above mapmakers strove to create something with flair, something new, and something for you all to enjoy.

With great pleasure, I announce the release of the Project 42 levelpack!
The levelpack can be found in the 'Collection' thread, or right here.
I hope you all have a wonderful time playing.


Edit: Another thing, feel free to post in any of the threads in this subforum. That's what they're here for.

#227456 8 2006-09-12T01:50:13Z krusch
I think mikey210's advice (about drawing inspiration from all those Metanet maps you have at your disposal) is the best you've been given. When I make episodes, (virtually) the only maps I play between mapping sessions are LittleViking's Chronicles, Barabajagal's Episodium and Metanet's episodes. I find them very inspirational.

Another thing to consider is the levels' atmosphere/feel. I prefer a range of different styles and atmoshperes in my episodes, but rocket_thumped likes each episode to have a distinct theme, as demonstrated in the Echo pack. Different people like different themes (or different combinations of themes), so find what works for you.

Remember that your levels don't have to be epic in length, and often the simpler ones will be mos effective. Once you have all your levels made, a good thing to do is to go through them, and sort out which ones belong on Numa rather than in your episodes. Do this based on map length/difficulty/jumperness. Jumpers aren't fun.

Hope that can help you at least a little, and good luck with your episodes.

Im sorry i can't see a hand there, its probably blantently obvious.
Well, it's a hand that's formed into a faux gun, like this, but with one finger as the barrel, rather than two.

#289119 8 2007-01-23T12:28:23Z krusch
Quote: maximo, 2007-01-23T05:36:50Z
Top rated pages next: Obviously this screws the hell out of the current system based of highest scores and percentages. I think the new table should work as follows though. The top rated page is broken into 3 categories: past week, past month, and all time (with the possibility of past 24 hours if deemed appropriate). The maps with the most Yay's for the given time block are listed in descending order. This in my oppinion gives the advantage of a frequently changing top authors page, giving new authors a chance to be seen, and letting numa cycle and be more fluid than it is now (Mount Doom is still up there). Although Mount Doom is probably worthy of the spot, when you are surfing for new maps seeing a 2 year old map isn't want you want to see quickly. This allows quicker scanning of numa and a higher chance new authors will be recognized (or at least in theory).

Quoted for emphasis. I think this is one of the best suggestions I have ever heard.

#367814 30 2007-09-27T07:07:04Z krusch
Critique time.


A Day in the Life - Fantastic, difficult but fun. It would make a good mid-to-late column episode finisher, I think. I don't think anything needs changing, and I find that bottom left switch is only awkward if you come at it from the ground below. I find walljumping from the gold in the bottom corner works well.

A Foreign Land - The launch pad chamber, I would strongly consider changing. The rest of the map: fun! I spent ages down the bottom, trying to jump from the trapdoor to the gold on the right before I realised that I uhh, didn't need to. Stupid tktktk map conditioning.

At Peace in my Castle - Perfect.

Attain Wave - One I'm less fond of, but I'd put that down more as a question of my personal taste, rather than the quality of the map.

Be Bold - Bounceblocks and drones are fun. Map's kind of sedate, which I don't think is really a bad thing. I got a smooth 110.775 :D

Black Market - Cute mini-map. Though, I find it extremely difficult to make the gold worth the time it takes to get it, what with timing the laser right.

Chockful - Awesome concept and execution. I think maximo's right about getting the switch though; it's a little too difficult as is.

Chutes and Jaggers - Excellent concept and execution again. No qualms this time, though. Fantastic stuff.

Conveniently Hidden - Again! Fantastic concept and execution.

Cowardice - " " . On top of that, I really love the whole getting out dilemma. It makes for a very frantic end when you don't notice it before you try.

Digital Age - Great easy map. It's the only one of yours my girlfriend's little brother could beat.

Eureka - At first I wasn't much a fan of this, because it seemed lacking in direction and structure. Then I played it some more, and some more, and I realised it's a lot of fun, and deceptively easy to get around in. Good map.

Exile - Solid map. Many more routes in there than there appears. The gold edit adds a little more class, too.

Fatigue Sets In - So much fun! Nothing wrong with this.

Final Transmission - Not your greatest creation, but the use of one-ways is so so smart. Sometimes the two bounceblocks cuss me out when I'm trying to jump big: I suggest maybe replacing each pair with one bounceblock snapped in between the tileses.

Firewall - Excellent stuff. Falls neatly into the category of "thwump wall", but those side sections save it from becoming clich. And, it reminds me of those old stepself episodes which are the bomb.

Hangime - Good. Difficult.

Huddle - Great! I love the style.

Hung up on Options - Yeah, good use of lasers, good balance of difficulty between all-gold and completion, and the pointy bits remind me of LV too. No problems.

I.Never.Knew - Nice map, now that I understand it. 99.100 :D

Interlock - Very smooth movement for such a tight little, uh, map. The floorguard section makes the difficulty inconsistent, though. I edited: here, but does that make it too easy? Use it if you like it.

Jagged Halls - Great! Love the concept, and the optional gold was good. Mines were used very well too.

Jello - Works very well.

Jones - The climbing technique is neat, but the gold doesn't feel worth the effort.

Journeyman - Argh! This is so fantastic.

Like a Bridge - Reasonably good. Reminds me of Vehemence maps, I guess in that it's minimalistic. I like the way the focus is on the launch pads.

Lure - Good, but incredibly challenging. I haven't beaten it yet. I'd consider dumbing this down a little before release, unless there's something I'm missing.

Megaquake - Mm. Excellent.

Monster Waves - Absolutely fantastic! Those gausses give great coverage, and the bounceblock jumps are tough, and the mines! Eeek! Every enemy is used just perfectly. I'd give this 5 on NUMA.

Neosurrealism - Good, but not great.

Pedestal - I like this more than Neosurrealism, but it's still not a stand-out. I just noticed, the top central tiles remind me of my first map for The Epic.

Perimeter - One of my least favourites of yours. It's an okay concept, but the map lacks direction, and I don't find the gameplay any fun. I'm sorry.

Persistent, Aren't They? - Ahh! Wonderful!

Pier - Decent.

Reverse Icarus - Excellent map, concept, title.

Roomrunner - t


And that's as much as I'd backed up before I lost the post. I was up to Vortex. Luckily, I kept my edit for Vortex on the clipboard.

Here it is.

Maybe you like it?


Anyway, I'll redo the rest when I feel less discouraged.

#368066 15 2007-09-28T13:08:11Z krusch
Quote: Animator, September 28, 2007 05:56 am
Hmm... since floorguards were ment to be a long range enemy, they where made to move to the ninja at alomst any distance, but when he's too close, they totally ignore the ninja.

Of course, this is just a guess that I have tested based on the fact that any time I move farther from the floorguard in that glitch example, it hunts me down like a dog I am.
Hm, no, it's not that exactly, but you're on the right track.

I'm not very good at explaining things like this, so I'll used these slightly edited words:

QUOTE (iangb @ June 21, [B)
2005[/B] 08:33 pm
]I think that it is because the floorguard algorithm checks every tile that the floorguard can go to, to see if the ninja is there (if it is there, then the floorguard moves as far as possible in that direction) ... the ninja is on the same tile as the one the floorguard is on so it is not detected, because the floorguard doesn't check its own tile (programming that adjustment in would be possible, but I can see why M&R thought there was no need in the first place).

I think this is probably the most accurate explanation for the glitch, but, like, Mare () or someone with a better knowledge of the game's mechanics can feel free to contradict it.

#369096 4 2007-10-02T15:33:24Z krusch
I don't like idea 1, because simple challenge maps or "trick" maps tend to have one route that's impossible to beat, and the highscores for them become static after a while (see 59-0: wtf?!).

I rather like idea 2, and I think it could be very popular. I also think races lend themselves to being arranged episodically better than puzzles or simple challenge maps, which tend to have minimal gold and a playability limit, so to speak.

I don't like idea 3 for the same reason as for idea 1. Puzzles just aren't as good for highscoring as other maps.

I really don't like idea 4, because those kinds of things never work out.


If you're looking to fill columns, I suggest you go for maps that have already been arranged episodically. I gathered some good sets:

- The Completed Column
- The first column of Baraba's Episodium
- stepself's Summer's End Column (minus those eight at the end, of course)
- The Camisade Collection (yes I know I was in it)

Of course, you'll have to remember to contact all of the makers before you use them. Some might object.

#373703 19 2007-10-22T14:47:22Z krusch
Quote: Topolord, October 22, 2007 12:52 pm
Quote: krusch, October 21, 2007 07:27 pm
Quote: Turtle, October 21, 2007 11:09 am
my average rating is still a 3.5 because of that. nobody cares. just make good maps, and you will be overall enjoyed. rating does not matter.
Three point five is a good rating. It's above "above average". Every time you or anyone speaks like it's not, you diminish the "cues" Sweep and maestro put up to try and save the rating system, and it frustrates me incredibly. Please!
psh.

Ratings are absolutely relative. A 3.5 to one person might be a great rating, but to another it might be crap. Don't act like you're "saving the rating system" by lowering the average rating. In fact, people should keep rating the way they always have. The system can differentiate between a 4.1 average and a 4.2, so there's no reason to change the way you rate. unless you're sniping or anti-sniping. then you should stop.
No. The ratings system is not relative anymore, it is standardized. That is the point.

I never said lowering your average given rating was going to save the ratings system, either. I said that sticking to the standardized rating system was going to save it, and I believe it will.

However, applying the rating standards probably means lowering the average given rating for a lot of people, as I think more people overrate than underrate. But I would argue the other way if so was the case.

#381014 50 2007-11-19T23:58:31Z krusch
Battle of Mice are a "post-metal" band with some beautiful melodic guitar and Julie Christmas' distinct screams, Eluvium is a solo ambient pianist/composer-y guy, maudlin of the Well were a wondeful progressive metal band that I've only really got into recently, Slint were a melancholy post/math rock band from the early '90s.

Some of you will have heard of some of those, but they're all great and definitely underappreciated.

#385964 5 2007-12-04T01:25:21Z krusch
We shouldn't be doing this until the year is over, but:

Arcade Fire - Neon Bible - I've always loved Arcade Fire. While I can't *really* say I think this album is one of '07's best, I did enjoy it immensely, so it makes the list.

Coheed and Cambria - Good Apollo, I'm Burning Star IV, Volume 2: No World for Tomorrow - Absolutely awesome prog rock/metal album.

Crippled Black Phoenix - A Love of Shared Disasters - If just for the third and fourth songs, this makes the list.

Do Make Say Think - You, You're a History in Rust - Cute and creative post-rock.

Dream Theater - Systematic Chaos - If it weren't for this album, they wouldn't be touring and I wouldn't be seeing them. Woo!

Eluvium - Copia - Magnum opus?

Grizzly Bear - Friend EP - I wish they would release more NEW material, rather than remixing their old stuff. *sigh*. Still a great EP.

jesu/Eluvium - Split - Two of my favourite artists! Absolutely great.

Minus the Bear - Planet of Ice - My least favourite of theirs, which says more about the band than the album.

Pelican - City of Echoes - A nice post-metal release.

Stars of the Lid - And Their Refinement of the Decline - Sleepy.


I highlighted the best ones.

#386803 10 2007-12-06T07:19:15Z krusch
I had this same problem up until about July this year, when my parents got a hold of my first semester school report. Feeling the consequences of this kind of behavior really helped me to reverse it, which I feel I have done successfully.

I think facing up to some of the people you've been misleading (like your parents or teachers) is a good if you really do want to reform. However, if you couldn't do this directly (because it's very difficult, I know), you could set yourself up to be caught out somehow. Self-betrayal can work both ways.


Remember that this is the way I solved my problem, so by no means will it definitely work for you, and Yanni and others have better advice anyway. But I can empathize with you. It's a really tough situation to be in.

Edit: southpaw: that's a defence mechanism. It's called regression, and while can be problematic, it does work nicely, hehe.

#391845 33 2007-12-21T06:17:52Z krusch
maximo: the entire point of Banzai Wipe-Out is to ride the wave of thwumps created when the ninja falls. Your edit misses that entirely.

Also, why do you and Blizz have such an aversion to mineless or goldless maps? I guess I'm okay with your hors d'oeuvres edit, although it loses the concept (the map's supposed to be goldless and cold, like hors d'oeuvres). But I am not okay with your edits for mary maudlin and Who Invented Acid Baths? I'll go over them here:

mary maudlin: Why? Why? Why? This is supposed to be simple, easy, and traversible. Your mines clog it up pointlessly, close off the best route (the top), and make the map pointlessly harder: Zenith needs easy maps! Leave it as I had it or cut it, please. No mines.

Who Invented Acid Baths?: This thing is ruined conceptually. Originally, the hard jump over the rocket was entirely optional, for accomplished players, etc. Your edit seemed to miss this jump, as the gold you put above the exit switch necessitates walljumping up (which is stupid) as well as placing the exit switch there, making this compulsory. In the original, newbies would have to make a tight jump under the rocket if they wanted to get to the left wall quicker, or they would ride down the right wall and walljump up the left: the original tiles made simply holding left out of the first door suicide. Unless they were game to jump across for that central piece of gold and a much quicker time. Again, please use the original or don't use this at all.
